LowRemote Desktop policy weaker than the baselinerdp_policy_gaps · Remote Access5 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. RDP is enabled and its policy explicitly allows a weaker security layer or encryption level, does not require a password prompt or secure RPC, or leaves drive redirection on (data can be copied out of the session).
Attacks it enables. Weaker RDP sessions: low encryption or legacy security layers allow interception, saved passwords allow logon without knowing them, and drive redirection lets data and tools move through the session.
Remediation. Set Remote Desktop Session Host policies: security layer SSL, encryption level High, always prompt for a password, require secure RPC, and do not allow drive redirection.

LowApp Installer (winget) is not restricted per the CIS Windows Server baselineapp_installer_policy_gaps · System Hardening3 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. An App Installer / Windows Package Manager policy the CIS Windows Server benchmark asks for and Windows 11 does not: the local archive malware scan can be overridden (EnableLocalArchiveMalwareScanOverride not 0), the Microsoft Store source certificate pinning can be bypassed (EnableBypassCertificatePinningForMicrosoftStore not 0), or the winget command-line interfaces are enabled (EnableWindowsPackageManagerCommandLineInterfaces not 0, Level 2). Each widens how software can be installed on a server. Unset counts, because the default leaves them enabled.
Attacks it enables. Malicious package installation: a user or malware installs software through winget from a local or unpinned source, past the usual software controls.
Remediation. Through Group Policy (Windows Components > Desktop App Installer) disable the local archive malware scan override and the Microsoft Store certificate pinning bypass, and, where winget is not needed on the server, disable the Windows Package Manager command-line interfaces.

LowAutoPlay / AutoRun not fully turned offautoplay_enabled · System Hardening3 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. AutoPlay is not disabled for all drives, or AutoRun commands are not blocked, so a USB stick or disc can start or suggest running its content.

LowRemote access tool installedremote_access_tool · Remote Access2 finding(s) on 2 host(s)
Why it matters. A remote access or remote management tool is installed (AnyDesk, TeamViewer, RustDesk, ScreenConnect, Splashtop, VNC, Radmin, NetSupport, Atera, MeshCentral and others). Each is a way into the host from the internet that bypasses the VPN and often the firewall, and attackers install the same tools after they get in, because they look legitimate. Name the ones the organization approves with -AllowedRemoteTools: the others are Medium. Without that list every tool found is Low, to be verified.

LowBitLocker policy differs from the CIS BitLocker profilebitlocker_policy_gaps · Disk and Boot2 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. A BitLocker Group Policy value is set and differs from the CIS optional BitLocker profile: recovery options and their escrow to AD DS, data recovery agent, smart cards or passwords on data drives, TPM startup key and PIN combinations, access from earlier versions of Windows. Unset counts.
Attacks it enables. Easier evasion on servers: local users can read the Defender exclusion list to know where to hide, and quick scans skip excluded paths an attacker may use.

LowRemote Credential Guard is not in useremote_credential_guard_off · Credential Protection2 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. On a domain-joined host: as a Remote Desktop client, no policy makes it use Remote Credential Guard ("Restrict delegation of credentials to remote servers" set to Require Remote Credential Guard or Restrict credential delegation), so an administrator's credentials are sent to every server they connect to; as a Remote Desktop host, it does not accept Remote Credential Guard (AllowProtectedCreds = 1, also a CIS Level 1 rule). An Essential Eight Maturity Level Three requirement.
Attacks it enables. Credential theft on the server side of a Remote Desktop session: a compromised server captures the administrator's credentials or hash and reuses them (pass-the-hash, lateral movement).
Remediation. Set "Remote host allows delegation of non-exportable credentials" to Enabled and "Restrict delegation of credentials to remote servers" to Require Remote Credential Guard (Computer Configuration > Administrative Templates > System > Credentials Delegation). Remote Credential Guard needs Kerberos and direct connections (no RD Gateway).

LowLogon screen shows the last user or skips CTRL+ALT+DELinteractive_logon_gaps · System Hardening2 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. The logon screen displays the last signed-in user name (a free account list for anyone at the keyboard), or the secure attention sequence is not required, which makes credential-stealing fake logon screens easier.
Attacks it enables. Credential capture and reconnaissance: the logon screen gives away the last user name, and without CTRL+ALT+DEL a fake logon screen can collect passwords.
Remediation. Set "Interactive logon: Don't display last signed-in" to Enabled and "Do not require CTRL+ALT+DEL" to Disabled.
MITRE ATT&CK. T1087
CIS Windows 11 Enterprise v3.0.0. rule 2.3.7.1, 2.3.7.2 (L1)
CIS Windows 10 Enterprise v3.0.0. rule 2.3.7.1 (L1); 2.3.7.2 (L1)
SeverityHostObjectFoundRequiredDetail
LowWKS-ACCT-017CTRL+ALT+DEL not required10DisableCAD = 1 (CIS Windows 10 2.3.7.1 expects 0).
LowWKS-ACCT-017Last signed-in user shown01DontDisplayLastUserName = 0 (CIS Windows 10 2.3.7.2 expects 1).
Fix script 2 of 2 finding(s) · read it, run it elevated in Windows PowerShell 5.1; it changes nothing until $Apply = $true 
# Registry values. Windows PowerShell 5.1, elevated.
$Apply = $false   # set to $true to apply; with $false it only shows what would change
$changes = @(
    @{ Path = 'HKLM:\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Policies\System'; Name = 'DontDisplayLastUserName'; Value = 1; Type = 'DWord' }
    @{ Path = 'HKLM:\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Policies\System'; Name = 'DisableCAD'; Value = 0; Type = 'DWord' }
)
foreach ($c in $changes) {
    $key = Get-Item -LiteralPath $c.Path -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
    $vn = if ($c.Name -eq '(default)') { '' } else { $c.Name }   # the default value is '' to the registry API
    $old = if ($key -and ($key.GetValueNames() -contains $vn)) { $key.GetValue($vn) } else { '(not set)' }
    '{0}\{1}: {2} -> {3}' -f $c.Path, $c.Name, $old, $(if ($c.Type -eq 'MultiString') { '(empty)' } else { $c.Value })
    if ($Apply) {
        if (-not $key) { New-Item -Path $c.Path -Force | Out-Null }
        Set-ItemProperty -LiteralPath $c.Path -Name $c.Name -Value $c.Value -Type $c.Type
    }
}
# Undo: set each value back to the "old" printed above, or Remove-ItemProperty it if it was (not set).
Group Policy or Intune can set a value back: fix it there if the host is managed. Test on a pilot host first.
LowServer network setting differs from the CIS Windows Server baselineserver_network_policy_gaps · Network Exposure2 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. A network setting the CIS Windows Server benchmark asks for and Windows 11 does not: the default IPv6 DNS servers are still used (DisableIPv6DefaultDnsServers not 1, Level 2), or Internet Explorer feeds send Basic authentication in clear text over HTTP (AllowBasicAuthInClear not 0), which exposes the credentials on the wire. Unset counts, because the default is the weaker state.
Attacks it enables. Interception: IPv6 default DNS servers can be spoofed (mitm6), and RSS feeds can send credentials in clear text with Basic authentication.
Remediation. Set "Turn off default IPv6 DNS Servers" to Enabled (DisableIPv6DefaultDnsServers = 1) and "Turn on Basic feed authentication over HTTP" to Disabled (AllowBasicAuthInClear = 0) through Group Policy.

Why it matters. NBT-NS poisoning works like LLMNR poisoning. "Default" means enabled unless DHCP turns it off.
Attacks it enables. Credential capture by name poisoning: an attacker on the local network answers NetBIOS name lookups (Responder) and collects NTLM hashes to crack or relay.

LowWindows Firewall shows notifications when it blocks a programfirewall_notifications_on · Network Exposure2 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. The firewall policy of a profile explicitly sets DisableNotifications = 0. CIS expects the notification off (1): a pop-up lets a standard user be talked into allowing a program through, and the firewall rule is created by the user, not by the administrator.
Attacks it enables. Social engineering: when the firewall blocks a program, the user is asked to allow it, and malware that triggers the prompt may simply be allowed.
Remediation. Set "Windows Firewall: <profile>: Settings: Display a notification" to No for the Domain, Private and Public profiles.

LowGuests or local accounts not denied network or remote logondeny_logon_rights_missing · Local Accounts2 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. The deny rights for network, batch, service, local and Remote Desktop logon do not list Guests (and, for network and Remote Desktop, "Local account"), so the Guest account or a local account can log on remotely if it is ever enabled or reused. Member servers deny network logon to "Local account and member of Administrators" instead; domain controllers, which have no local accounts, only need Guests.
Attacks it enables. Lateral movement with local accounts: an attacker who has one local password or hash (often the same on many hosts) signs in over the network or RDP; the Guest account can be used too.
Remediation. Add Guests to the five deny logon rights and Local account to "Deny access to this computer from the network" and "Deny log on through Remote Desktop Services" (on member servers: "Local account and member of Administrators group" for network logon; on domain controllers Guests only).

LowPreinstalled games, consumer apps or antivirus trialsbloatware_installed · Software1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. Store apps and programs that a managed business device rarely needs are installed or provisioned for new users: preinstalled games (Candy Crush, Solitaire, Xbox), consumer apps (TikTok, Spotify, Disney+, Netflix, Clipchamp, Bing News), and third-party antivirus trials (McAfee, Norton) that compete with Microsoft Defender and expire. Each is more code that updates on its own schedule, talks to the internet and can carry vulnerabilities; an expired antivirus trial can leave Defender in passive mode. The list is curated in lib\Checks.ps1 (OEM driver and firmware utilities are left out on purpose). Low.
Attacks it enables. Larger attack surface: preinstalled apps and trials update on their own schedule, reach the internet and carry their own vulnerabilities (games and adware have shipped remote code execution flaws), and an expired third-party antivirus can leave Defender in passive mode.
Remediation. Remove what the organization does not use, for the installed users and from the provisioned list so new profiles do not get it back (Remove-AppxPackage -AllUsers, Remove-AppxProvisionedPackage -Online, or the uninstaller of the program), and keep it out of the image. HostBadger's guided menu offers the removal as an explicit choice.
MITRE ATT&CK. T1176
SeverityHostObjectFoundRequiredDetail
LowWKS-ACCT-017preinstalled apps2 foundnoneKing games (Candy Crush...) (installed); Microsoft Solitaire Collection (provisioned for new users).
LowStricter Authenticode signature check not enabled (CVE-2013-3900)authenticode_padding_check_off · System Hardening1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. EnableCertPaddingCheck is not 1: Windows accepts a signed file with extra data appended after its signature, so a signed installer can carry an attacker's payload and still show as signed (CVE-2013-3900, in the CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities catalog). Microsoft left the fix opt-in because some installers built that way then look unsigned.
Attacks it enables. Signed-file tampering: an attacker appends a payload to a validly signed executable (CVE-2013-3900) and Windows still reports the signature as valid.
Remediation. After testing the installers you deploy, set EnableCertPaddingCheck = "1" under HKLM\Software\Microsoft\Cryptography\Wintrust\Config and, on 64-bit Windows, HKLM\Software\Wow6432Node\Microsoft\Cryptography\Wintrust\Config, then restart. No fix script: an installer that breaks needs a decision.
MITRE ATT&CK. T1553.002
SeverityHostObjectFoundRequiredDetail
LowWKS-ACCT-017Authenticode padding checknot set1EnableCertPaddingCheck is not 1 under Wintrust\Config and Wow6432Node\...\Wintrust\Config (opt-in fix for CVE-2013-3900).
LowWPAD auto-discovery is not disabledwpad_not_disabled · Network Exposure1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. Web Proxy Auto-Discovery is not turned off: DisableWpad is not 1 and the WinHTTP Web Proxy Auto-Discovery service (WinHttpAutoProxySvc) is not disabled. Clients keep looking for a "wpad" host through DNS, LLMNR, mDNS and NBT-NS, and whoever answers becomes their proxy and can capture or relay NTLM authentication.
Attacks it enables. Traffic interception and credential capture: an attacker answers WPAD discovery, becomes the host's web proxy and collects NTLM authentications.
Remediation. Set HKLM\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Services\WinHttp\Parameters\DisableWpad = 1 (or disable the WinHttpAutoProxySvc service) where proxy auto-discovery is not used, and keep "wpad" in the DNS global query block list.
MITRE ATT&CK. T1557.001

LowWKS-ACCT-017WPAD auto-discoveryDisableWpad = 0 and the WinHttpAutoProxySvc service is present: clients still resolve 'wpad', which an attacker can answer to capture NTLM.
LowOther attack surface reduction rules not in block modeasr_other_rules_not_blocking · Antivirus1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. Microsoft documents 16 more ASR rules beyond the standard three: Office child processes, code injection and executable content, obfuscated and downloaded scripts, executables from email or USB, PSExec/WMI process creation, Adobe Reader child processes, advanced ransomware protection, rebooting into Safe Mode, copied or impersonated system tools, and (judged only where Exchange Server is installed) web shell creation. The finding lists the rules that are not set to Block.

LowWebClient (WebDAV) service is enabledwebclient_webdav_enabled · Network Exposure1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. The WebClient (WebDAV Redirector) service is installed and not disabled. Where it runs, authentication coercion (PetitPotam, PrinterBug) can force the host to authenticate over HTTP instead of SMB, which is not protected by SMB signing and can be relayed to LDAP or AD CS web enrollment (ESC8). The service starts on a trigger, so Manual is enough to abuse. On a domain controller this is High (a relay to LDAP or AD CS is domain takeover).
Attacks it enables. Authentication coercion and NTLM relay: an attacker makes the host authenticate over HTTP (WebDAV, PetitPotam) and relays it to LDAP or AD CS, which SMB signing does not stop.
Remediation. Set the WebClient service to Disabled where WebDAV is not needed (or remove the WebDAV Redirector feature), and enforce it through Group Policy (System Services).

SeverityHostObjectDetail
LowWKS-ACCT-017service: WebClientWebClient (WebDAV) is Stopped, start mode Auto: coercion (PetitPotam) can force the host to authenticate over HTTP for NTLM relay.

LowBitLocker without pre-boot PINbitlocker_tpm_only · Disk and Boot1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. The OS volume unlocks with the TPM alone. On a stolen laptop, TPM-sniffing and DMA attacks can recover the key; a PIN closes them.

LowEvent logs are not sent anywherelog_forwarding_missing · Logging1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. No Windows Event Forwarding subscription manager is set by policy, no known log shipping agent runs (Splunk Universal Forwarder, Elastic Agent or Winlogbeat, NXLog, Wazuh, Azure Monitor Agent, QRadar WinCollect, Graylog Sidecar, Fluent Bit and others) and no EDR agent sends telemetry. The logs stay on the host, where an attacker with administrator rights clears them (wevtutil cl Security) and where nobody looks during an incident. Low on a workstation, Medium on a server or domain controller.
Attacks it enables. Covering tracks: an attacker with administrator rights clears the local logs and nothing of the intrusion remains elsewhere.
Remediation. Forward the Security, System, PowerShell and Sysmon logs to a collector: Windows Event Forwarding (Group Policy: Configure target Subscription Manager) or the agent of your SIEM. If the host uses an agent HostBadger does not know, accept the finding with an exception.

    'Defender {0}: {1} -> {2}' -f $c.Setting, (Get-MpPreference).($c.Setting), $c.Value
    if ($Apply) { $p = @{ $c.Setting = $c.Value }; Set-MpPreference @p }
}

Low.NET Framework 3.5 (2.0 and 3.0) is enablednetfx3_enabled · System Hardening1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. The optional feature NetFx3 is enabled. .NET 2.0 and 3.x lack the AMSI and the security features of .NET 4.8, and attack tools target them to dodge both. The Essential Eight wants it disabled or removed at Maturity Level Three.
Attacks it enables. Security bypass by downgrade: tools run on .NET 2.0/3.5, which has no AMSI, so in-memory .NET payloads are not scanned.
Remediation. Find the applications that still need .NET 3.5, then turn the feature off (Disable-WindowsOptionalFeature -Online -FeatureName NetFx3, or Remove-WindowsFeature NET-Framework-Core on servers). It is a compatibility decision: there is no fix script.
MITRE ATT&CK. T1562.010
ACSC Essential Eight. User application hardening, ML3: .NET Framework 3.5 (includes .NET 2.0 and 3.0) is disabled or removed.
SeverityHostObjectFoundRequiredDetail
LowWKS-ACCT-017.NET Framework 3.5enableddisabled or removedThe NetFx3 optional feature is enabled.

Why it matters. Neither a title nor a text is configured for the message shown before logon. A legal notice supports acceptable-use and monitoring policies.
Attacks it enables. Legal exposure rather than a direct attack: without a warning banner, prosecuting or disciplining unauthorized use can be harder.

LowSecurity event log too smallsecurity_log_small · Logging1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. The Security log rolls over quickly at this size; events from the start of an intrusion are gone before anyone looks, unless they are forwarded.
Attacks it enables. Lost evidence: the Security log rolls over quickly, so logon and privilege events of an intrusion are overwritten before anyone looks.
Remediation. Set the Security log maximum size to at least 196,608 KB (CIS), more on servers and DCs, and forward events to the SIEM.

Fix script 1 of 1 finding(s) · read it, run it elevated in Windows PowerShell 5.1; it changes nothing until $Apply = $true 
# Registry values. Windows PowerShell 5.1, elevated.
$Apply = $false   # set to $true to apply; with $false it only shows what would change
$changes = @(
    @{ Path = 'HKLM:\SOFTWARE\Policies\Microsoft\Windows\EventLog\Security'; Name = 'MaxSize'; Value = 196608; Type = 'DWord' }
)
foreach ($c in $changes) {
    $key = Get-Item -LiteralPath $c.Path -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
    $vn = if ($c.Name -eq '(default)') { '' } else { $c.Name }   # the default value is '' to the registry API
    $old = if ($key -and ($key.GetValueNames() -contains $vn)) { $key.GetValue($vn) } else { '(not set)' }
    '{0}\{1}: {2} -> {3}' -f $c.Path, $c.Name, $old, $(if ($c.Type -eq 'MultiString') { '(empty)' } else { $c.Value })
    if ($Apply) {
        if (-not $key) { New-Item -Path $c.Path -Force | Out-Null }
        Set-ItemProperty -LiteralPath $c.Path -Name $c.Name -Value $c.Value -Type $c.Type
    }
}

LowSystem tools used to download payloads can reach the networklolbin_outbound_not_blocked · Network Exposure1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. No enabled Windows Firewall rule blocks outbound traffic for system tools that attackers use to download payloads or run remote scripts (certutil, mshta, wscript, cscript, regsvr32, wmic, bitsadmin). Rules in the local store and in Group Policy are read; rules pushed by Intune (MDM store) are not, and an outbound default of Block is not considered.
Attacks it enables. Living-off-the-land downloads: an attacker uses signed Windows tools (certutil -urlcache, mshta or regsvr32 /i pointed at a remote URL, bitsadmin) to fetch payloads and run remote scripts past application allow lists.
Remediation. Add outbound block rules for the listed programs (System32 and SysWOW64) where nothing legitimate needs them online, through Group Policy, Intune or the fix script. Check first that management tools do not depend on them (bitsadmin and wmic in some deployment scripts).
MITRE ATT&CK. T1105
SeverityHostObjectFoundRequiredDetail
LowWKS-ACCT-017outbound: system toolscertutil.exe, mshta.exe, wscript.exe, cscript.exe, regsvr32.exe, wmic.exe, bitsadmin.exeblocked outbound7 of 7 tools have no outbound block rule: certutil.exe, mshta.exe, wscript.exe, cscript.exe, regsvr32.exe, wmic.exe, bitsadmin.exe.
LowServer setting differs from the CIS Windows Server baselineserver_baseline_gaps · System Hardening1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. A setting that only the CIS Windows Server benchmarks ask for: the server can be shut down from the logon screen without signing in, a member server unlocks with cached credentials instead of asking a domain controller (a disabled or changed account can still unlock it), or Windows LAPS, backing up to Active Directory, does not enforce the password expiration (an administrator can push the expiry out and keep the same password).
Attacks it enables. Weaker server defenses: shutdown or unlock without proper logon, or LAPS passwords that stay valid past their expiry, give an attacker or insider an easier path.
Remediation. Set "Shutdown: Allow system to be shut down without having to log on" to Disabled; on member servers set "Interactive logon: Require Domain Controller authentication to unlock workstation" to Enabled (the server then needs a domain controller to unlock); in the Windows LAPS policy enable "Do not allow password expiration time longer than required by policy".
MITRE ATT&CK. T1078
CIS Windows Server 2019 v4.0.0. rule 2.3.13.1 (L1)
SeverityHostObjectFoundRequiredDetail

LowPrograms can run from removable disksremovable_media_execute_allowed · System Hardening1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. The Removable Storage Access policy does not deny execute access to removable disks ("Removable Disks: Deny execute access", Deny_Execute = 1) and does not deny all removable storage (Deny_All). Programs on a USB drive or SD card run like any other. Write and read access are an organizational choice and are only reported in the detail.
Attacks it enables. Removable-media infection and insider attacks: a program brought on a USB drive or SD card (a dropped "found" stick, a rubber-ducky payload, an unapproved tool) runs directly from the device.
Remediation. Set "Removable Disks: Deny execute access" (Computer Configuration > Administrative Templates > System > Removable Storage Access) where users have no need to run programs from USB, and keep the ASR rule that blocks untrusted processes from USB in Block mode.
MITRE ATT&CK. T1091
SeverityHostObjectFoundRequiredDetail
LowWKS-ACCT-017Removable disksnot configuredDeny_Execute = 1Removable Disks: Deny execute access is not configured and Deny_All is not set.
LowBrowser extension can read and change every websitebrowser_extension_broad_access · Software1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. An enabled browser extension has access to every website (<all_urls>, *://*/* or a content script on every page): it can read what the user types and sees on any site, mail and banking included, and change it. Many useful extensions need it (ad blockers, password managers), which is why this is Low, to be verified: the risk is the publisher, or a buyer of the extension who pushes a malicious update. The detail names the sensitive APIs it also uses (cookies, webRequest, debugger, proxy, nativeMessaging, scripting...). Reported only without -AllowedExtensions; extensions pushed by policy are not reported.
Attacks it enables. Session theft through a trusted extension: an update (or a new owner) of an extension that already reads every site steals cookies and passwords, mail and banking included.
Remediation. Keep only the extensions people need, from publishers the organization trusts, and name them with -AllowedExtensions. Enforce the list in the browser (ExtensionInstallAllowlist / ExtensionSettings) and, where possible, restrict the sites extensions can touch (runtime_blocked_hosts in ExtensionSettings).
MITRE ATT&CK. T1176
SeverityHostObjectDetail
LowWKS-ACCT-017extension: Page Translator Plus (eeeeffffgggghhhhiiiijjjjkkkkllll)Can read and change every website; also uses cookies, scripting (no -AllowedExtensions list: verify that it is approved). Installed in: Brave mbianchi/Profile 1.
LowMemory integrity (HVCI) not runninghvci_off · Credential Protection1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. Hypervisor-protected code integrity is not running, so a vulnerable or malicious kernel driver is not blocked from loading. It needs virtualization-based security and compatible drivers; on a virtual machine without nested virtualization it cannot run.
Attacks it enables. Kernel attacks: without memory integrity, malicious or vulnerable drivers can load unsigned code into the kernel and disable security tools.
Remediation. Turn on Memory integrity (Windows Security > Device security > Core isolation, or the Group Policy "Turn On Virtualization Based Security") once the drivers are compatible.
MITRE ATT&CK. T1068
ACSC Essential Eight. Restrict administrative privileges, ML3: Memory integrity functionality is enabled.

# SMB. Windows PowerShell 5.1, elevated.
$Apply = $false   # set to $true to apply; with $false it only shows what would change
foreach ($c in @(
    @{ Side = 'Client'; Setting = 'RequireSecuritySignature'; Value = $true }
)) {
    $cfg = if ($c.Side -eq 'Server') { Get-SmbServerConfiguration } else { Get-SmbClientConfiguration }
    'SMB {0} {1}: {2} -> {3}' -f $c.Side, $c.Setting, $cfg.($c.Setting), $c.Value
    if ($Apply) {
        $p = @{ $c.Setting = $c.Value; Force = $true; Confirm = $false }
        if ($c.Side -eq 'Server') { Set-SmbServerConfiguration @p } else { Set-SmbClientConfiguration @p }
    }
}

LowPowerShell is not held to Constrained Language Modepowershell_clm_off · System Hardening1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. Nothing puts PowerShell in Constrained Language Mode: no enforced WDAC user-mode policy and no enforced AppLocker script rules (either makes PowerShell run untrusted code in Constrained Language Mode), and no __PSLockdownPolicy = 4. Full Language Mode lets a script call .NET and Win32 directly, which most PowerShell attack tooling needs. An Essential Eight Maturity Level Three requirement.
Attacks it enables. Fileless attack tooling: PowerShell scripts call .NET and Win32 directly (in-memory loaders, AMSI bypasses, credential dumpers written in PowerShell).
Remediation. Enforce WDAC or AppLocker script rules (Constrained Language Mode follows from them). The __PSLockdownPolicy environment variable alone is not a security boundary.
MITRE ATT&CK. T1059.001
ACSC Essential Eight. User application hardening, ML3: PowerShell is configured to use Constrained Language Mode.
SeverityHostObjectFoundRequiredDetail
LowWKS-ACCT-017PowerShellFull Language ModeConstrained Language Mode (WDAC or AppLocker script rules enforced)No enforced WDAC user-mode policy or AppLocker script rules: PowerShell runs in Full Language Mode.
LowNo EDR or endpoint protection agent foundedr_not_found · Antivirus1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. None of the services of the well-known EDR and endpoint protection products (CrowdStrike, SentinelOne, Defender for Endpoint, Carbon Black, Cortex XDR, Sophos, Trend Micro, Trellix, ESET, Symantec, Elastic, Cybereason, Cylance, Bitdefender, Huntress) is present. Microsoft Defender Antivirus alone is not counted as EDR. It is Low on a workstation and Medium on a server or domain controller. If you use another product, name it with -ExtraEdrServices, or declare the one you expect with -ExpectedEdr.
Attacks it enables. Blind spot: nothing on the host detects or responds to attacker activity beyond basic antivirus.
Remediation. Install the EDR your organization uses. If the host does run one that HostBadger does not recognise, pass its service name with -ExtraEdrServices so that it is counted.

LowMulticast DNS (mDNS) enabledmdns_enabled · Network Exposure1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. The host answers and asks multicast DNS on the local network, on by default. Like LLMNR and NetBIOS name resolution, it lets anyone on the segment answer a name lookup (Responder) and collect NTLM hashes or relay them.
Attacks it enables. Credential capture by name poisoning: an attacker on the local network answers mDNS lookups and collects NTLM hashes to crack or relay.
Remediation. Set "Configure multicast DNS (mDNS) protocol" to Disabled (HKLM\SOFTWARE\Policies\Microsoft\Windows NT\DNSClient EnableMDNS = 0), together with LLMNR and NetBIOS over TCP/IP.

LowMany domain logons cachedcached_logons_high · Credential Protection1 finding(s) on 1 host(s)
Why it matters. Cached domain credentials (MSCache v2 hashes) can be extracted and cracked offline from a stolen or compromised machine. Servers rarely need them at all.
Attacks it enables. Offline cracking of domain passwords: an administrator on the host dumps the cached domain logons (DCC2 hashes, mimikatz lsadump::cache) and cracks them; the more are kept, the more accounts are exposed.
Remediation. Set "Interactive logon: Number of previous logons to cache" to 4 or fewer on workstations and 0-1 on servers.
